## Learning Knowledge Pack (2026-05-02 user-driven research delta)

This section is a user-requested research synthesis appended to the prior `Engine knowledge baseline (auto-curated 2026-05-02)` and its `Engine knowledge baseline refresh (2026-05-02, scheduled-task delta)`. It is additive only: it does not re-state the .NET 1 → 11 evolution, the four forecast horizons, the 12 first-class engine qualities, or the 12 NuGet shipping families, all of which the prior baseline already carries. Instead, it fills the specific gaps that surfaced when surveying the docs graph alongside the May 2026 software-world state — detailed C# 15 / ASP.NET Core 11 / EF Core 11 / Aspire 13 deltas not yet in the durable baseline, the explicit GoF + DDD + resilience + cloud-native pattern catalog mapped onto Cephalon's existing surfaces, the explicit ISO/IEC 25010:2023 + OWASP ASVS 5.0 + NIST SSDF v1.1 final plus SSDF v1.2 draft readiness input + SLSA v1.1 + Sigstore mapping for the 12 ilities, the OpenTelemetry semantic-convention discipline the engine should adopt, an SRE SLO + error-budget posture for the engine itself, and a ranked set of high-leverage recommendations to advance the engine across the next slice. Cross-references at the end of this section route depth back into the durable baseline and into `docs/long-range-direction.md`, `docs/engineering-standards.md`, `docs/architecture-patterns-research.md`, and `docs/architecture/design-patterns-reference.md` so this section stays a navigable summary rather than a parallel source of truth.

### Verification posture for this pack

- baseline pin still confirmed as `.NET SDK 10.0.201` via `global.json`, shipping target framework `net10.0`, package version baseline `0.1.0-preview`; as re-verified against official Microsoft sources on `May 7, 2026`, `.NET 10` remains the LTS shipping floor (`10.0.7` latest observed patch from `April 21, 2026`, support through `November 14, 2028`) and `.NET 11 Preview 3` (`April 14, 2026`, SDK `11.0.100-preview.3.26207.106`) remains the latest Microsoft preview, with Preview 4 still expected on or near the second Tuesday of May 2026 per Microsoft's monthly cadence
- primary research sources consulted for this pack: `learn.microsoft.com`, `devblogs.microsoft.com/dotnet`, `github.com/dotnet/core`, `github.com/dotnet/aspnetcore`, `github.com/dotnet/efcore`, `github.com/microsoft/aspire`, `andrewlock.net`, `microservices.io`, `martinfowler.com`, `cncf.io`, `opentelemetry.io`, `csrc.nist.gov`, `owasp.org`, `slsa.dev`, `sigstore.dev`, `iso.org`, `quality.arc42.org`, `sre.google`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu`, `w3.org`, `gartner.com`, `thoughtworks.com`
- this section is user-driven, not a scheduled-task run; it is logged outside the `Scheduled-task run log` subsection and should be refreshed in place when (a) the next .NET preview lands, (b) any of the cited frameworks publishes a new revision (ISO 25010, ASVS, SSDF, SLSA, OTel semconv stable), (c) any recommendation below is promoted from "noted" into a shipped engine primitive (in which case the resulting fact moves into the durable baseline and the recommendation shrinks or is removed here)

### `.NET 11` / C# 15 / ASP.NET Core 11 / EF Core 11 / Aspire 13 deltas not yet captured in the durable baseline

The durable baseline already names Runtime Async, Zstandard in `Microsoft.AspNetCore`, ReadyToRun devirtualization of non-shared GVMs, faster `uint`-to-float/double casts, switch-expression branchless folding, and EF Core 11 JSON-mapped complex types on TPT/TPC inheritance. The additional deltas Cephalon should keep on the readiness radar:

- **C# 15 union types (preview)** — closed, exhaustive sum types with compiler-enforced switch coverage, expected to ship with .NET 11 GA; the language plus runtime story is still moving across previews. Cephalon public-API design should avoid type names that collide with `union` / `IUnion` / `UnionAttribute` and should design return shapes so they can grow into `union` types additively (a `OneOf<TSuccess, TFailure>` style result in transport adapters and behavior dispatch is an obvious upgrade candidate once the language feature is final)
- **C# 15 collection expression arguments (preview)** — pass constructor / factory args alongside `[ ... ]` collection expressions; relevant for engine-side immutable descriptor builders and source-generated catalog initializers
- **ASP.NET Core 11 async validation in Minimal APIs**, plus union-type flow through Minimal APIs and SignalR parameter binding — `Cephalon.AspNetCore` REST mapping and the JSON-RPC / GraphQL / SignalR-adjacent adapters should plan an additive opt-in once GA ships rather than a pre-GA bet
- **OpenAPI binary file response shape** for both Minimal APIs and controllers, plus continued OpenAPI 3.1 + JSON Schema 2020-12 generation under Native AOT — `Cephalon.AspNetCore.OpenApi` (or the future generator surface) should mirror this once the engine claims OpenAPI export rather than emitting a pre-3.1 shape
- **`IOutputCachePolicyProvider`** for dynamic policy resolution — useful as the resilience / policy companion stack matures and the engine grows declarative cache descriptors
- **SignalR auth-token refresh + correlation-id tracking** — relevant once a real-time-experience companion pack actively claims SignalR runtime ownership rather than only descriptor truth
- **Kestrel TLS handshake reduction + HTTP/3 timing improvements** — ambient wins; the engine just needs to keep `KestrelServerOptions` / `IConfigureOptions<KestrelServerOptions>` extensibility intact and not freeze TLS defaults inside engine code
- **EF Core 11** — `MaxByAsync` / `MinByAsync`, `EF.Functions.JsonPathExists()` and `JsonContains()` (SQL Server 2022+ / 2025), `VectorSearch()` + `HasVectorIndex()` (SQL Server 2025), `SqlVector<T>` lazy-loading default (reported up to 9× local / 22× remote speedups), complex types + JSON in Cosmos DB, transactional-batches-by-default + opt-in bulk + session-token APIs in Cosmos. Engine guidance: do not bake any of these into the public contract of `Cephalon.Data` / `Cephalon.EventSourcing`; surface them through provider companion packs so the abstraction stays portable across providers that lack vector or JSON-path support
- **EF Core 11 NativeAOT + precompiled queries** (`dotnet ef dbcontext optimize --precompile-queries --nativeaot`) — Microsoft still recommends against deploying EF NativeAOT to production, so Cephalon should keep this on the readiness lane only and not promote any `Cephalon.Data` claim to AOT-trim-truthful until the harness verdict supports it
- **EF Core 11 migration ergonomics** — `ExcludeForeignKeyFromMigrations()`, `dotnet ef database update --add` (compile-and-apply via Roslyn), `--connection` / `--offline` for `migrations remove` / `database drop`, latest-migration ID embedded in model snapshot for branch-merge conflict detection — generated-app baselines and CI integration should plan to surface these without leaking provider-specific flags into engine descriptors
- **`.NET 11` container base images are signed** (Preview 3) — Cephalon's container-image publishing path should propagate signature verification through `docs/container-image-publishing.md` rather than masking it; chiseled Ubuntu / Azure Linux distroless variants remain the recommended baseline (`*-chiseled-aot`, `*-chiseled-extra`)
- **Aspire 13 / 13.1** is the live cadence (not "Aspire 11"); 13.1 added MCP integration, Azure Data Lake Storage / `Aspire.Hosting.Azure.Network` (VNet/NSG/private endpoints), MongoDB EF Core, Bun, Certbot integrations. Engine integration with Aspire should follow Aspire's release line, not the .NET runtime line; treat `IHostApplicationBuilder` as the convergent host shape and avoid pinning new code to the older `IHostBuilder` surface
- **`Microsoft.Extensions.AI` + `Microsoft.Extensions.VectorData`** — the engine's agent / tool surface (`Cephalon.Agentics`) should plan to interoperate with these extension contracts as they stabilize, rather than building a parallel AI-provider abstraction; this hedges against per-vendor agent runtimes (Bedrock AgentCore, Vertex AI Agent Builder, Foundry Agent Service) becoming the dominant integration surface
- **`dotnet run` for file-based apps + `#:include`** — pivot toward script-like single-file scenarios; relevant for `Cephalon.Cli` ergonomics and for the `dotnet new` template surface

### Pattern catalog mapped onto Cephalon's existing surfaces

The durable baseline lists the patterns Cephalon has already encoded as runtime surfaces. The catalog below is the explicit cross-walk for the patterns commonly cited in the wider .NET / cloud-native body of knowledge — what Cephalon already implements, hosts behind, or should keep on the radar:

- **GoF — Strategy** ⇄ behavior dispatch + transport profiles + provider selection in `Cephalon.Data` / `Cephalon.Eventing`; the canonical engine pattern for "swap algorithm via DI"
- **GoF — Decorator** ⇄ behavior pipeline composition (`Cephalon.Behaviors.*`), middleware ordering in `Cephalon.AspNetCore`, observability decorators on `IRuntime`
- **GoF — Chain of Responsibility** ⇄ behavior topology dispatch (`BehaviorDispatcher`), the resilience pipeline once phase-11 lands, validation pipelines
- **GoF — Mediator** ⇄ in-process behavior dispatch with typed request/response; engine prefers explicit `IBehaviorContext` over a generic mediator base type so consumers do not pay for an extra abstraction layer
- **GoF — Adapter** ⇄ host adapters (`Cephalon.AspNetCore`, `Cephalon.Worker`, future `Cephalon.WebAssembly`), transport adapters (REST / JSON-RPC / gRPC / GraphQL / SSE / WS), provider adapters in `Cephalon.Data.*`, observability exporters
- **GoF — Factory / Abstract Factory** ⇄ `IRuntimeFactory`, scaffolding generators, project templates, package-loading factories
- **GoF — Observer** ⇄ runtime catalog change notifications, `/engine/snapshot` consumers; current shape is `snapshot.*` polling, observable streams (`IObservable<T>` / `IAsyncEnumerable<T>`) are a possible additive future
- **DDD — Aggregate / Aggregate Root** ⇄ application-managed in module business logic; engine intentionally stays neutral on aggregate shape and only provides marker hints (analyzer rules `[AggregateRoot]` / `[ValueObject]` are a low-cost future addition)
- **DDD — Value Object** ⇄ application-managed; engine guidance: prefer C# `record` / `record struct`, immutable, equatable, no primitive obsession in module-public APIs
- **DDD — Repository** ⇄ application-managed on top of `Cephalon.Data` providers; engine does not impose a generic `IRepository<T>` because that abstraction tends to leak provider semantics and obscure query intent
- **DDD — Bounded Context + Context Mapping** ⇄ Cephalon modules + technology profiles + cell-based architecture; the engine offers structural primitives (modules, cells, capability descriptors) consumers can use to express bounded contexts without forcing DDD vocabulary into the engine surface; the seven context-map relationships (shared kernel, customer-supplier, conformist, anticorruption, open host service, published language, separate ways) all map to existing Cephalon descriptors or to consumer-managed adapters
- **DDD — Anti-Corruption Layer** ⇄ transport adapters and behavior translators; consumers can layer ACLs as additional behaviors or adapter wrappers
- **Resilience — Retry / Circuit Breaker / Bulkhead / Timeout / Hedging / Rate Limit** ⇄ planned phase-11 resilience primitives in the engine, today realized through Polly v8 pipelines and `Microsoft.Extensions.Resilience` in consumer code; the engine should expose declarative resilience descriptors that compose with behavior topology rather than wrapping Polly directly
- **Reliability — Idempotency Key + Inbox / Dedupe Window** ⇄ `Cephalon.Eventing` and `Cephalon.Agentics` already touch this; a host-agnostic `IIdempotencyKey` plus pluggable store is a natural additive engine primitive
- **Data — Transactional Outbox** ⇄ shipped in `Cephalon.Data`; the engine must keep the outbox transport-agnostic and not couple to a specific broker
- **Data — CDC / Log-tailing** ⇄ shipped through `Cephalon.Data` outbox + provider-native CDC pumps (Postgres logical decoding, SQL Server CDC, Debezium adapter); aligns with the 2026 trend that the database itself is increasingly the event stream
- **Data — Materialized View / Read Model Projection** ⇄ event-sourcing projections in `Cephalon.EventSourcing`
- **Data — Event-carried State Transfer vs. Event Notification** ⇄ engine-neutral; consumers choose per coupling / consistency tradeoff and the engine should keep both shapes first-class in eventing descriptors
- **Coordination — Saga Orchestration vs. Choreography** ⇄ saga choreography is shipped (`AsSagaChoreography()`); orchestration (process manager) remains future work and should land additively as `AsSagaOrchestration()` rather than rewriting the choreography path
- **Coordination — Durable Execution** ⇄ shipped (`AsDurableExecution()`); the engine should keep the contract abstract enough to plug a Dapr / Temporal / Restate / DBOS runtime without rewriting consumer code
- **Cloud-Native — Sidecar / Ambassador / Service Mesh integration** ⇄ planned for `Cephalon.Edge` and the `service-mesh-integration` technology surface
- **Cloud-Native — Strangler Fig + Backend-for-Frontend** ⇄ shipped descriptors (`Engine:Migration:StranglerFig`, `Engine:BackendForFrontend`); good examples of "additive runtime metadata expressing an architectural intent" without forcing one shape on consumers
- **Cloud-Native — Cell-based Architecture** ⇄ shipped; cell boundary, cell routes, cell health isolation, traffic automation
- **Cloud-Native — Feature Flags** ⇄ shipped (`Engine:Features`); the engine should integrate with `Microsoft.Extensions.FeatureManagement` rather than re-inventing flag evaluation
- **Cloud-Native — WASI 0.2+ Component Model / WebAssembly host** ⇄ on the long-range horizon; the engine should not assume the substrate (managed CLR vs WebAssembly) at the contract layer

### Quality framework mapping for the 12 first-class engine qualities

The durable baseline names the 12 qualities (Performance, Security, Usability, Reliability, Maintainability, Scalability, Flexibility, Compatibility, Data Integrity, Availability, Auditability, Compliance) but does not map them to external frameworks. The mapping below gives planning cards and reviewers a single matrix; once `docs/engineering-standards.md` carries the full table, this section should shrink to a back-pointer.

- **Performance** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010:2023 *Performance Efficiency* (time behaviour, resource utilisation, capacity); enforced through BenchmarkDotNet guardrails, `<EnablePackageValidation>` for binary-perf-affecting changes, allocation discipline (`Span<T>` / `Memory<T>` / source generators / `LoggerMessage`); CI publishes p95 / p99 / alloc-per-op as artifacts
- **Security** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Security*; OWASP ASVS 5.0 (≈350 requirements across ≈17 categories, CRE-mapped) for the consumer-facing security floor; OWASP AISVS for AI-touching surfaces; NIST SSDF v1.1 final plus SP 800-218 Rev. 1 / SSDF v1.2 draft readiness input (Prepare-Org / Protect-Software / Produce-Well-Secured / Respond-to-Vulnerabilities) plus SP 800-218A for the GenAI development process; Cephalon should map each `Cephalon.Audit` / `Cephalon.MultiTenancy.Governance` / `Cephalon.Identity` claim to specific ASVS requirement IDs in `docs/engineering-standards.md`
- **Usability** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010:2023 *Interaction Capability* (the renamed Usability characteristic); enforced through `cephalon doctor`, generated starter output, scaffolding ergonomics, low-code shorthand (`AddRestBehaviorModule<TMarker>`), and the explicit "less code at adoption" goal
- **Reliability** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Reliability*; SRE SLI / SLO posture (see below); resilience pipeline (retry / circuit breaker / bulkhead / timeout); `runtime-failure-policy.md` startup / stop semantics
- **Maintainability** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Maintainability* (modularity, reusability, analysability, modifiability, testability); analyzer pack, `PublicApiAnalyzers`, central package management, deterministic builds, doc-comment discipline
- **Scalability** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Performance Efficiency:Capacity* and *Flexibility:Scalability* sub-characteristics; cell-based architecture, behavior topology decoupling, runtime catalog read-only paths
- **Flexibility** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Flexibility* (the new top-level characteristic absorbing *Portability* and adding *Adaptability*, *Scalability*, *Installability*, *Replaceability*); host-agnostic abstractions, transport-agnostic behaviors, swappable providers, the explicit goal that consumers change architecture without rewriting application code
- **Compatibility** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Compatibility*; SemVer 2.0 + .NET binary compatibility rules; `<PackageValidationBaselineVersion>`; `[Obsolete]` migration with `DiagnosticId` and `UrlFormat`
- **Data Integrity** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Reliability:Maturity* + *Security:Integrity*; outbox / inbox / idempotency contracts; CDC pump correctness; event-sourcing snapshot semantics
- **Availability** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Reliability:Availability*; SRE SLO posture; `/health/live` / `/health/ready` semantics; cell health isolation; deployment-mode survivability claims
- **Auditability** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Maintainability:Analysability* + *Security:Accountability*; `Cephalon.Audit`, `tenancy.governance-action.*` decisions, runtime introspection (`/engine/*`, `snapshot.*`); aligned with EU CRA Article 13 vulnerability-handling logs and CRA Annex II conformity evidence
- **Compliance** ⇄ ISO/IEC 25010 *Functional Suitability:Functional Appropriateness* + cross-cutting; aligned with EU CRA enforcement (main provisions binding `December 11, 2027`, reporting obligations from `September 11, 2026`), EU AI Act high-risk and GPAI obligations (high-risk waves `August 2, 2026` and `August 2, 2027`; GPAI compliance `August 2, 2027`), W3C DID v1.1 / EUDI Wallet acceptance in regulated EU sectors from `2027`, NIST CSF, GDPR/CCPA, HIPAA where applicable
- **Supply-chain** (cross-cutting through Security + Compliance + Auditability) ⇄ SLSA v1.1 — Cephalon should target **L3** for its own packages (hermetic builds, signed provenance, transparency log); Sigstore (Cosign + Fulcio + Rekor) for keyless signing tied to OIDC identity; SBOM in CycloneDX (and SPDX as a translation if a downstream consumer requires it) per package; NuGet Trusted Publishing replacing long-lived API keys

### OpenTelemetry semantic-convention discipline for the engine

- the engine's first-class telemetry contract is `System.Diagnostics.ActivitySource`, `System.Diagnostics.Metrics.Meter`, and `Microsoft.Extensions.Logging.ILogger` — never invent a parallel telemetry abstraction
- all emitted span / metric / log attribute names must match OpenTelemetry semantic conventions (HTTP, DB, Messaging, RPC, runtime); GenAI semantic conventions are still stabilising through 2026, so the engine should follow the OTel stability proposal that decouples *instrumentation* stability from *semconv* stability rather than freezing names prematurely
- attribute cardinality is part of the contract; the engine should treat unbounded high-cardinality dimensions (full request URLs, raw user input, full SQL strings) as a defect in instrumentation, not a feature
- structured-logging discipline: `LoggerMessage` source generator for hot paths; `EventId`s aligned with the per-package diagnostic-id ranges; never log secrets / PII / authentication tokens; redaction filters applied at the engine boundary, not the consumer boundary
- OTLP export configuration is the responsibility of host adapters and observability companion packs; engine code stays exporter-agnostic so the same telemetry can fan out to the Aspire dashboard, Grafana, Datadog, Honeycomb, or a future agentic observer

### SRE SLO + error-budget posture for the engine itself

- the engine has reliability semantics worth committing to: cold-start time, allocation per dispatch, AOT compile time, NuGet restore time, reference-doc generation time, release-validation time, flake rate of layered tests
- proposed engine-level SLIs (initial draft, refine after the next benchmark guardrail run): `p95 minimal-API cold start ≤ X ms`, `p95 BehaviorDispatcher dispatch latency ≤ Y ms`, `alloc-per-dispatch ≤ Z bytes`, `scripts/validate-release.ps1 wall time ≤ W minutes`, `dotnet restore wall time ≤ V seconds with lock-mode`, `flake rate of tests/* projects ≤ 0.5%`
- error-budget policy: when an SLI burns its budget faster than threshold, freeze new feature merges on the affected family until the budget recovers; reuse the `architecture-review-YYYY-MM-followups.md` shape to track burn-down so SLO breaches do not dissolve back into the monthly snapshot
- the SRE posture is additive: the engine does not impose SLOs on consumer applications; it provides the SLI primitives (BenchmarkDotNet guardrails, `cephalon doctor` reports, runtime introspection) that consumer SRE teams can compose into their own SLO targets

### Long-range signals to absorb beyond the four-horizon forecast

The durable baseline already commits to a 4-horizon forecast (3-5y / 5-10y / 10-20y / 20-30+y). The signals below are research notes, not commitments, that the assistant should keep on the radar when the forecast horizons are next refreshed:

- **WASI 1.0 / Component Model finalisation (2026 onwards)** ⇄ keep `Cephalon.Engine` and `Cephalon.Abstractions` substrate-agnostic so a future `Cephalon.WebAssembly` host lands additively; never assume `System.IO`-style ambient authority in core APIs
- **Capability-based security mainstream (10y)** ⇄ design file / net / secret / key access as opaque capability handles even when today they wrap `System.IO`, so a future enclave-backed or WASI-capability implementation is a swap, not a rewrite
- **Confidential computing baseline (5y; Gartner forecasts >75% of operations on untrusted infra secured in-use by TEEs by 2029)** ⇄ keep secret + key handles opaque; keep cryptographic abstractions pluggable for hybrid post-quantum + classical signing
- **W3C DID v1.1 + EUDI Wallet (mandated acceptance from 2027 in regulated EU sectors)** ⇄ keep `IPrincipalContext` / claims surface principal-agnostic; avoid baking "JWT subject string" into core types so OIDC, mTLS, DID/VC, and eIDAS can all flow through the same primitive
- **Carbon-aware scheduling + FinOps + GreenOps merging (CNCF Kepler, Green Software Foundation patterns)** ⇄ provide carbon / cost / locality / latency-budget metadata as additive runtime descriptors that platform schedulers can read regardless of K8s vs WASI-fabric vs successor
- **AI-coded volume + cognitive-debt risk (ThoughtWorks Tech Radar v34, April 2026)** ⇄ keep public APIs small enough to be amenable to both AI and human review; tag APIs `[Stable]` / `[Evolving]` / `[Experimental]` / `[Speculative]` so analyzers can police references; resist generated-code dumps from accumulating into the engine core
- **Durable execution + agentic platforms convergence** ⇄ keep durable-execution context and agent-tool registries abstract so Dapr, Temporal, Restate, DBOS, Bedrock AgentCore, Vertex AI, and Foundry Agent Service can all implement them as adapters
- **EU Cyber Resilience Act enforcement (`December 11, 2027`)** ⇄ emit machine-readable conformity evidence (vulnerability handling logs, SBOM, SLSA provenance, `[Obsolete]` deprecation pathway) by default; treat compliance as a byproduct of design rather than a sprint
- **Programmable observability via eBPF + OTel pipelines (8y)** ⇄ keep telemetry as `ActivitySource` / `Meter` / `ILogger` so future query-pushdown collectors can compute on the wire without engine code changes
- **Post-quantum hybrid TLS (NIST ML-KEM / ML-DSA, expected to mature by `.NET 12` LTS)** ⇄ keep cryptographic primitives behind `IConfigureOptions<>` / provider-managed seams; never freeze suite preferences in engine code

### Decision invariants worth designing around (15–30 year constants)

- speed-of-light latency, packet loss, partial failure (the "8 fallacies of distributed computing") still hold — bake `IAsyncEnumerable` / `Channel<T>` / back-pressure into core
- CAP / PACELC trade-offs in distributed state — never offer a single "consistent + available + partition-tolerant" abstraction; force callers to pick
- end-to-end principle for trust and correctness — checksums, idempotency keys, authoritative validation belong at the engine's responsibility boundary, not in hop-by-hop infra
- Conway's law — package boundaries should match team / ownership boundaries; cross-package cycles cost more than duplication (Cephalon's family taxonomy already follows this)
- Hyrum's law — assume every observable becomes a contract; deliberately hide what you do not want frozen (internal types, JSON field ordering, log shapes, runtime catalog field order beyond the documented surface)
- Postel's law nuance — be liberal in *parsing* but strict in *generating*; tolerate forward-compat input but never emit ambiguous output
- Lehman's laws of software evolution — continuing change, increasing complexity, conservation of familiarity; staff anti-rot work continuously and treat refactoring as a planned activity, not a heroic rescue

### Top high-leverage recommendations to advance the engine across the next slice

These are research-output recommendations, not committed engine work; they should be turned into `ENG-*` planning cards through `docs/engine-backlog.md` and `docs/planning-governance.md` rather than implemented from this section directly. Each recommendation names the engine quality dimension it most directly advances so reviewers can evaluate the claim per the existing `docs/engineering-standards.md` discipline.

1. **Stand up the public-API contract lock-in** (Compatibility, Maintainability) — *consolidated into the supply-chain uplift plan as `ENG-322` (Sprint 117) on `Cephalon.Abstractions` first; see `docs/supply-chain-uplift-plan.md`*
2. **Make every shipping package AOT- and trim-clean by default** (Performance, Flexibility, Compatibility) — set `<IsAotCompatible>true</IsAotCompatible>` on packable projects, replace remaining runtime reflection with source generators (logging, JSON, options binding, DI registration), keep trim/AOT analyzer warnings at error severity, and keep the `not-claimed` posture honest until `scripts/validate-deployment-mode-claims.ps1` returns `claim-truthful`
3. **Adopt NuGet lock files + package source mapping in CI** (Compatibility, Security, Auditability) — *consolidated into the supply-chain uplift plan as `ENG-321` (Sprint 116); see `docs/supply-chain-uplift-plan.md`*
4. **Sign and attest releases — target SLSA v1.1 L3** (Security, Compliance, Auditability) — *consolidated into the supply-chain uplift plan as `ENG-324` (Sprint 119); see `docs/supply-chain-uplift-plan.md`*
5. **Ship `Cephalon.Diagnostics` as the OpenTelemetry semantic-convention adapter** (Auditability, Reliability, Maintainability) — *consolidated into the supply-chain uplift plan as `ENG-323` (Sprint 118); see `docs/supply-chain-uplift-plan.md`*
6. **Map the 12 first-class engine qualities to ISO/IEC 25010:2023 + ASVS 5.0 + SSDF v1.1 final / SSDF v1.2 draft readiness input + SLSA v1.1** (Compliance, Auditability, Maintainability) — *shipped through `ENG-318` as the Quality framework mapping section in `docs/engineering-standards.md`*; per-surface ASVS requirement-ID mapping for `Cephalon.Audit`, `Cephalon.MultiTenancy.Governance`, and `Cephalon.Identity` claims remains the next-slice follow-through
7. **Promote a small `Cephalon.Resilience` primitive** (Reliability, Availability, Data Integrity) — *shipped through `ENG-390` (May 4, 2026, PR #907)*: declarative retry / circuit-breaker / bulkhead / timeout / hedging / rate-limit descriptors live in [`src/Cephalon.Abstractions/Resilience/*`](../src/Cephalon.Abstractions/Resilience/) and [`src/Cephalon.Abstractions/AppModel/*Selection.cs`](../src/Cephalon.Abstractions/AppModel/), and the engine-managed resilience runtime now lives in [`src/Cephalon.Resilience/Resilience/*`](../src/Cephalon.Resilience/Resilience/) backed by `Microsoft.Extensions.Resilience` (Polly v8 in-box). The `BehaviorResiliencePolicyResolver`, `BehaviorCircuitBreakerStateRegistry`, `DefaultBehaviorResilienceExceptionClassifier`, and `BehaviorResilienceExecutionContextKeys` types moved with git-tracked rename history preserved at 92-99% similarity; the residual `BehaviorResilienceExecutionMiddleware`, `BehaviorIdempotencyResolver`, and `BehaviorResilienceRuntimeCatalog` types stayed in `Cephalon.Behaviors` because moving them would have required widening currently-internal surface or created circular project references. Tracked as `M2 — mixed application-managed descriptors plus cephalon-managed runtime` split across `Cephalon.Resilience` and `Cephalon.Behaviors` in [`docs/engine-surface-maturity-audit.md`](engine-surface-maturity-audit.md#current-audit). Promotion to `M3` is gated on an explicit operator surface (catalog routes, snapshot keys) landing inside `Cephalon.Resilience`. The engine does not re-implement what `Microsoft.Extensions.Resilience` already covers.
8. **Land the trim/AOT/single-file claim story end-to-end** (Compatibility, Performance, Auditability) — `scripts/validate-deployment-mode-claims.ps1` exists, `representativePublishTargets.projects` is populated with five sample hosts, the expanded set publishes cleanly under `singleFile`, publish probes restore any `packages.lock.json` churn they create, `Cephalon.Diagnostics` is the first clean-baseline package-scoped `singleFile` claim with explicit project properties, `Cephalon.Data.MySql.SciSharpReplication` and `Cephalon.ReferenceDocs` are explicit package-level `not-claimed` support boundaries, and the harness now emits manifest-backed policy/inventory truth through `PublishProbePolicy`, `HazardInventory`, and `hazard-inventory.json`; the remaining gap is to remove `-SkipPublish` from release validation only when publish probing should become a gate and to widen global deployment-mode rows only when the intended package set, workflow, docs, and package guidance agree
9. **Define engine-level SLOs and an error-budget policy in `docs/sre-posture.md`** (Reliability, Maintainability) — *shipped through `ENG-319` as the engine SRE SLO + error-budget posture baseline*; verifying the initial SLO targets against the next clean benchmark guardrail run, wiring per-SLI baseline pass/fail signal into `scripts/validate-release.ps1`, and flipping the deployment-mode claim truthfulness SLI from `audit-only` to numeric remain the next-slice follow-through
10. **Author `Cephalon.Analyzers` as a curated meta-package** (Maintainability, Security, Compatibility) — *consolidated into the supply-chain uplift plan as `ENG-325` (Sprint 120); see `docs/supply-chain-uplift-plan.md`*

- `docs/package-publishing.md` and `docs/external-package-lifecycle.md` — NuGet publication design (the future home of trusted-publishing + provenance + SBOM uplift)
- `docs/deployment-mode-support.md` and `scripts/validate-deployment-mode-claims.ps1` — trim / AOT / single-file claim truth
- `docs/test-coverage-roadmap.md` — layered-test posture and gap inventory

### Refresh cadence for this Learning Knowledge Pack

- refresh in place when a new .NET preview lands, when ISO/IEC 25010 / OWASP ASVS / NIST SSDF / SLSA / OTel semconv publish a new revision, or when any pattern listed above is promoted from "noted" into a first-class engine primitive
- never duplicate the durable baseline above; if a recommendation here is converted into shipped engine work, fold the resulting fact into the durable baseline section and shrink (or remove) the recommendation here
- this section is meant to stay a navigable summary of *external research the assistant has internalised*; it is not a change log and should not accumulate dated run entries — those belong in the `Scheduled-task run log` subsection of the prior baseline refresh

## Provider completeness hardening note (2026-05-07)

- treat the `Cephalon.Observability.*Dependencies` provider packs as `M2` / `provider-managed` rather than `M0` taxonomy-only: each shipped dependency pack owns a concrete managed probe loop, provider timeout/failure behavior, diagnostics event ids, cached `IDependencyHealthContributor` truth, and `/engine/dependencies` / readiness integration; promotion beyond this needs operator automation, live reconciliation, or adoption evidence unique to a provider
- treat the fourteen `Cephalon.Observability` cloud / exporter / logging packs as `M1` / `cephalon-managed` runtime-truth projections rather than managed telemetry collectors: the baseline `Cephalon.Observability` pack projects `telemetry-export-guidance`, OpenTelemetry and the twelve cloud/platform packs project `telemetry-export-*`, and Serilog projects `logging-provider-serilog` through the shared `observability` technology surface with sanitized metadata that redacts endpoints, headers, tokens, connection strings, and credential material
- treat the seven multi-tenancy outbound sender/token-provider packs (`HttpDelivery`, `SmtpDelivery`, `SendGridDelivery`, `MailgunDelivery`, `AmazonSesDelivery`, `MicrosoftGraphDelivery`, and `MicrosoftGraphDelivery.AzureIdentity`) as provider-managed runtime-truth contributors: each projects a sanitized `tenant-invitation-delivery-*` technology surface with active endpoint/channel/credential-flag/posture metadata while redacting API keys, access tokens, passwords, webhook-signing secrets, header values, query secrets, and credential identifiers
- treat `Cephalon.EventSourcing` provider completeness as an `M1` runtime-truth hardening, not an `M2` promotion: all ten provider packs now contribute sanitized append/read store descriptors through `IEventStoreContributor`, `IEventStoreCatalog`, and the `event-sourcing` runtime surface; promotion still waits for provider-owned managed projection/rebuild/snapshot/retention/replay lifecycle
- treat the event-sourcing stream-version contract as zero-based across every provider pack: `GetVersionAsync` returns `-1` for a missing stream, `AppendAsync(..., expectedVersion: -1)` persists the first event as `StreamVersion = 0`, replay defaults from `fromVersion = 0`, and tooling coverage now rejects provider docs or entry-model comments that reintroduce 1-based language
- treat non-relational `Cephalon.Data` provider completeness as provider-visible runtime truth, not catalog-only: MongoDB, Redis, Neo4j, Cassandra, ClickHouse, Elasticsearch, OpenSearch, Qdrant, and NATS can now be active in one engine with provider outbox/inbox descriptors projected through `IOutboxCatalog`, `IInboxCatalog`, and the `outbox-producers` / `inbox-stores` technology surfaces; URI-based providers redact inline user-info/query credentials in capability metadata, and shared capability families such as `data.search-store` aggregate `sourceModuleIds`, `providers`, `packs`, and `contributorCount` instead of failing composition when multiple providers expose the same family key
- treat relational `Cephalon.Data` provider completeness as shared provider-runtime truth: SQL Server, PostgreSQL, MySQL, and Oracle can now be active together, aggregate the shared `data.relational-store` capability family, and project provider-native CDC declarations plus execution runtime state into the `data-management` `cdc-captures` and `cdc-capture-runtimes` technology surfaces with sanitized metadata; the dedicated CDC integration lane now has opt-in live evidence for SQL Server, PostgreSQL, MySQL, and Oracle in addition to the default MongoDB replica-set lane; Entity Framework remains the owner of database role, migration, topology, and projection runtime surfaces, and EF test modules must register explicit `AddCephalonDataCommand` / `AddCephalonDataQuery` dispatch descriptors alongside handlers
- `2026-05-07 ENG-499` shipped Oracle live CDC integration evidence: `tests/Cephalon.Tests.CdcIntegration` now extends the external-service gate with `ExternalCdcServiceProvider.Oracle`, `CEPHALON_CDC_ORACLE_CONNECTION_STRING`, `ResolveOracleMode()`, and `OracleCdcIntegrationTests.OracleCdc_StagesOutboxAndCommitsCheckpointAgainstLiveLogMiner`; the test uses real `Cephalon.Data.Oracle` LogMiner execution, stages an outbox message from a live `ORDERS` row, asserts shared runtime-state and execution-runtime aggregation, verifies LogMiner payload/header metadata, and confirms durable `commitScn|changeScn|rsId|ssn` checkpoint persistence; Testcontainers mode bootstraps `gvenzl/oracle-xe:21.3.0-slim-faststart` with `ARCHIVELOG`, supplemental logging, and LogMiner privileges, while default CI remains Docker-free and skipped unless the external-service gate is enabled
- `2026-05-07 ENG-500` restored the regression-suite baseline across provider/runtime surfaces after full-suite validation exposed timing and parallel-resource flakes. Provider-native CDC hosting tests now run through a dedicated non-parallel xUnit collection, use deterministic suite-level wait windows, and still prove MongoDB, SQL Server, Postgres, MySQL, and Oracle runtime state through the shared CDC surfaces. The startup-failure health/diagnostics test now uses a 30-second manual restart-backoff window so it asserts failure-policy truth instead of racing the clock. Blueprint samples now register `Cephalon.Behaviors`, `Cephalon.Behaviors.Http`, and `Cephalon.Behaviors.SourceGen` so generated behavior REST endpoints materialize through the dispatcher at `/api/v1/*`; the matching sample docs, operations guide, and container-runtime validation script use the same versioned routes. Tooling package-publishing tests now drain stdout/stderr concurrently with a bounded timeout so the tooling suite cannot deadlock on child process output. The current validation anchor is `dotnet test .\CephalonEngine.slnx --no-restore`: Composition `807/807`, Hosting `750/750`, Tooling `357/357`, CdcIntegration `11` passed / `8` skipped by gate.
- `2026-05-07 ENG-501` shipped the provider integration canary baseline: `tests/Cephalon.Tests.ProviderIntegration` now contains an explicit external-provider gate (`CEPHALON_PROVIDER_EXTERNAL_SERVICES`, `CEPHALON_PROVIDER_TESTCONTAINERS`, `CEPHALON_PROVIDER_REDIS_CONNECTION_STRING`, plus ergonomic aliases) and `RedisProviderIntegrationTests.RedisProvider_StagesOutboxInboxDispatchAndEventStreamAgainstLiveRedis`. The Redis canary composes `Cephalon.Data.Redis`, `Cephalon.EventSourcing.Redis`, `Cephalon.Eventing`, and `Cephalon.Engine` together, then proves manifest capabilities, outbox/inbox descriptors, event-stream descriptor projection, Redis Hash/Sorted Set/Set/Stream persistence, outbox/inbox idempotency, dispatch-store success reporting, ordered event replay, and optimistic-concurrency rejection against a live Redis runtime when the gate is enabled. The closeout also hardened provider/runtime validation: relational provider-native CDC composition and hosting tests now accept the legitimate captured-to-idle transition while asserting capture totals/checkpoints, Tooling child-process tests drain stdout/stderr with bounded timeouts and run process-spawning tests non-parallel, Serilog request-log scope assertions wait for the expected event, behavior HTTP streaming timeout tests use a real timeout margin, and Kubernetes Gateway polling tests wait for expected automation state. Current validation anchor is `dotnet test .\CephalonEngine.slnx --no-build`: ProviderIntegration `11` passed / `3` skipped, CdcIntegration `11` passed / `8` skipped, Composition `807/807`, Hosting `750/750`, Tooling `357/357`; local Docker was unavailable, so the live Testcontainers Redis path compiled but was not executed on this machine.
